Actions on Objectives
Likely
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] PyPI install footprint of num2words v0.5.15/0.5.16 (Scavenger supply-chain compromise)
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Scavenger loader/stealer SHA256 execution or drop on endpoint · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Scavenger C2 callback: ifyouseethisyouareultragay[.]com / pokerainteasy[.]su · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] PyPI install footprint of num2words v0.5.15/0.5.16 (Scavenger supply-chain compromise) Delivery High
Detects developer/CI hosts that pulled the Scavenger-poisoned num2words releases. Hunts for pip/uv/poetry installing the exact compromised versions, and for the site-packages directory/_build.py loader DLL dropped by the malicious __init__.py. Both 0.5.15 and 0.5.16 were yanked by PyPI; any presence post-July-28 is actionable. Rationale: v0.5.15 and v0.5.16 are exact strings — the article names 0.5.15 as compromised; InvokeRE and OSV/MAL-2025-6794 confirm 0.5.16 was also published before being yanked. The malicious add-on is a single `_build.py` file inside the `num2words/` package directory (per InvokeRE reverse-engineering) — that file in that path is a uniquely high-fidelity drop. No FPs expected since both versions are gone from PyPI. Cross-checked against: • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-num2words-pypi-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://github.com/Invoke-RE/community-malware-research/blob/main/Research/Loaders/Scavenger/num2words_IOCs.md • https://osv.dev/vulnerability/MAL-2025-6794 • https://security.snyk.io/vuln/SNYK-PYTHON-NUM2WORDS-11172937 • https://blog.pypi.org/posts/2025-07-31-incident-report-phishing-attack/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.006
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
union
(DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName in~ ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","python.exe","python3.exe","uv.exe","poetry.exe")
    or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","python.exe","python3.exe","uv.exe","poetry.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("num2words==0.5.15","num2words==0.5.16","num2words-0.5.15","num2words-0.5.16")
    or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("num2words==0.5.15","num2words==0.5.16")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine),
(DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FolderPath has "site-packages" and FolderPath has_any (@"num2words-0.5.15", @"num2words-0.5.16"))
    or (FolderPath has @"\num2words\" and FileName =~ "_build.py" and InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("pip.exe","python.exe","python3.exe","uv.exe"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, FolderPath, FileName, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine)
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
(SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where (NewProcessName endswith @"\pip.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\pip3.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\python.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\python3.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\uv.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\poetry.exe")
| where CommandLine has_any ("num2words==0.5.15","num2words==0.5.16","num2words-0.5.15","num2words-0.5.16")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName),
(ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where TargetProcessName has_any ("pip","python","uv","poetry")
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("num2words==0.5.15","num2words==0.5.16","num2words-0.5.15","num2words-0.5.16")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName),
(ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where (TargetFilePath has "site-packages" and TargetFilePath has_any ("num2words-0.5.15","num2words-0.5.16"))
    or (TargetFilePath has @"\num2words\" and TargetFileName =~ "_build.py")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, TargetFileSHA256, ActingProcessName)
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: PyPI Install of Compromised num2words v0.5.15/0.5.16 (Scavenger)
id: 9c1c4d6e-6c8b-4f64-8e62-2ad2a9c4e7d0
status: experimental
description: Detects installation of the Scavenger-poisoned num2words PyPI package via pip/uv/poetry, including the exact yanked versions 0.5.15 and 0.5.16.
references:
  - https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/supply-chain-security-alert-num2words-pypi-package-shows-signs-of-compromise
  - https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-num2words-pypi-supply-chain-compromise/

author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2025/07/28
tags:
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.t1059.006
  - attack.initial_access
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_installer:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\pip.exe'
      - '\pip3.exe'
      - '\python.exe'
      - '\python3.exe'
      - '\uv.exe'
      - '\poetry.exe'
  selection_cmd:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - 'num2words==0.5.15'
      - 'num2words==0.5.16'
      - 'num2words-0.5.15'
      - 'num2words-0.5.16'
  condition: selection_installer and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
  - None expected — both versions were yanked from PyPI within hours of publication on 2025-07-28; any install attempt post-incident indicates a stale lockfile/cache or active compromise.
level: high
(source:windows.sysmon OR source:windows.security) (@EventID:1 OR @EventID:4688) (@Image:(*\pip.exe OR *\pip3.exe OR *\python.exe OR *\python3.exe OR *\uv.exe OR *\poetry.exe OR *\PIP.EXE OR *\PYTHON.EXE) OR @ParentImage:(*\pip.exe OR *\python.exe)) @CommandLine:(*num2words==0.5.15* OR *num2words==0.5.16* OR *num2words-0.5.15* OR *num2words-0.5.16*)
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.process_name IN ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","python.exe","python3.exe","uv.exe","poetry.exe","pip","pip3","python","python3") (Processes.process="*num2words==0.5.15*" OR Processes.process="*num2words==0.5.16*" OR Processes.process="*num2words-0.5.15*" OR Processes.process="*num2words-0.5.16*") by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | append [| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem where (Filesystem.file_path="*site-packages*num2words-0.5.15*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*site-packages*num2words-0.5.16*" OR (Filesystem.file_path="*site-packages*num2words*" Filesystem.file_name="_build.py")) by Filesystem.dest Filesystem.user Filesystem.file_path Filesystem.file_name Filesystem.process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`] | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.process_name IN ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","python.exe","python3.exe","uv.exe","poetry.exe","pip","pip3","python","python3") (Processes.process="*num2words==0.5.15*" OR Processes.process="*num2words==0.5.16*" OR Processes.process="*num2words-0.5.15*" OR Processes.process="*num2words-0.5.16*") by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | append [| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem where (Filesystem.file_path="*site-packages*num2words-0.5.15*" OR Filesystem.file_path="*site-packages*num2words-0.5.16*" OR (Filesystem.file_path="*site-packages*num2words*" Filesystem.file_name="_build.py")) by Filesystem.dest Filesystem.user Filesystem.file_path Filesystem.file_name Filesystem.process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`] | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
[LLM] Scavenger C2 callback: ifyouseethisyouareultragay[.]com / pokerainteasy[.]su Command & Control High
Detects egress to the two confirmed Scavenger loader C2 hosts referenced in InvokeRE's reverse-engineering of the num2words v0.5.15 loader. These hostnames are unique, lexically distinctive strings that should never appear in legitimate traffic — any DNS resolution or HTTP(S) connection is post-install-stage execution. Rationale: Both C2 strings are cross-confirmed in InvokeRE's reversing writeup and the Invoke-RE community IOC repo. Their lexical distinctiveness (one is a slur-laced novelty domain, the other a `.su` ccTLD) means zero collision with benign traffic — `has` match is sufficient. Sigma kept simple (single dns selection block). Cross-checked against: • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-num2words-pypi-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://github.com/Invoke-RE/community-malware-research/blob/main/Research/Loaders/Scavenger/num2words_IOCs.md
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1568
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Web.Web Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
union
(DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has_any ("ifyouseethisyouareultragay.com","pokerainteasy.su")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName),
(DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType == "DnsQueryResponse"
| extend QueryName = tostring(parse_json(AdditionalFields).QueryName)
| where QueryName has_any ("ifyouseethisyouareultragay.com","pokerainteasy.su")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, QueryName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine)
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
(DnsEvents
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where Name has_any ("ifyouseethisyouareultragay.com","pokerainteasy.su")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, ClientIP, Name, QueryType, ResultCodeName),
(CommonSecurityLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where DestinationHostName has_any ("ifyouseethisyouareultragay.com","pokerainteasy.su")
    or RequestURL has_any ("ifyouseethisyouareultragay.com","pokerainteasy.su")
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceVendor, SourceIP, DestinationIP, DestinationHostName, RequestURL, DeviceAction),
(ImDnsActivity
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where DnsQuery has_any ("ifyouseethisyouareultragay.com","pokerainteasy.su")
| project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, SrcHostname, DnsQuery, DnsResponseCodeName),
(ImWebSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where Url has_any ("ifyouseethisyouareultragay.com","pokerainteasy.su") or HttpHostName has_any ("ifyouseethisyouareultragay.com","pokerainteasy.su")
| project TimeGenerated, SrcIpAddr, SrcHostname, Url, HttpHostName, DvcAction)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Scavenger loader/stealer SHA256 execution or drop on endpoint Installation High
Hunts for the four confirmed Scavenger payload hashes (one loader + three stealer variants: electric/exodus/shiny) appearing as a file write, image load, or process execution on any endpoint. These hashes were extracted from the num2words v0.5.15 payload chain by InvokeRE. Catches the post-installation stages that drop version.dll/umpdc.dll/profapi.dll side-loading stealers targeting .pypirc, Electrum wallet config, and Exodus wallet binaries. Rationale: The four SHA256s are extracted directly from InvokeRE's reverse-engineering writeup and the Invoke-RE community IOC repository. SHA256 collisions on a hash sweep are essentially impossible, giving a near-zero FP rate. Covers post-install stages the StepSecurity article notes but doesn't enumerate — useful for hunting laterally if the install-stage UC1 has already fired on one host. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/Invoke-RE/community-malware-research/blob/main/Research/Loaders/Scavenger/num2words_IOCs.md • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-num2words-pypi-supply-chain-compromise/
ATT&CKT1574.002 T1555 T1552.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes
let scavenger_hashes = dynamic([
  "be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2",
  "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2",
  "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616",
  "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b"
]);
union
(DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes)
| project Timestamp, Source="DeviceFileEvents", DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName),
(DeviceImageLoadEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes)
| project Timestamp, Source="DeviceImageLoadEvents", DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName),
(DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes) or InitiatingProcessSHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes)
| project Timestamp, Source="DeviceProcessEvents", DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName)
| order by Timestamp desc
let scavenger_hashes = dynamic([
  "be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2",
  "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2",

  "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b"
]);
union isfuzzy=true
(ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes)
| project TimeGenerated, Source="ImFileEvent", DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine),
(ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where SHA256 in~ (scavenger_hashes)
| project TimeGenerated, Source="ImProcessCreate", DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, SHA256, ParentProcessName),
(SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where FileHash in~ (scavenger_hashes)
| project TimeGenerated, Source="SecurityEvent4688", Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine, FileHash, ParentProcessName)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b") or SHA1 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b") or MD5 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b") or SHA1 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b") or MD5 in~ ("be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b")

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["be917cb379b9622f56a4d5ec93bf00c20cb76c6646e5919690d0f7c09c956de2", "c2a7ee6ab9344e1bb13c61dc689d4a946678e0505367cd55c9b43ddee3d461e2", "439da8bb9c541d26b0f534b17d75790e252e4d9058561e8907f8690e21cd0616", "c36ebf96573afcb36bb31590d56e8af49502fb159e00fd4a59336f8a450bec8b"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

The GitHub Warning Everyone Ignores: 'This Commit Does Not Belong to Any Branch'

StepSecurity
2025-08-09 5 use cases 1 technique 5 kill-chain phases detected

Several popular GitHub Actions have release processes where the release commit does not belong to any branch on the action repository.

CVEsCVE-2025-30066 CVE-2025-30154
ATT&CKT1190
SHA10e58ed8671d6b60d… fbc2c5ebe64389f2… e1e36574b3af1dda… ce4a123414f9fffa… c17ac4b5c1cb901a…

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] tj-actions / reviewdog imposter-commit SHA referenced on CI/CD runner or developer host
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Article-specific behavioural hunt — The GitHub Warning Everyone Ignores: 'This Commit Does Not Belong to Any Branch'
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] tj-actions memdump.py credential-dump payload fetched or executed on CI runner
[LLM] tj-actions / reviewdog imposter-commit SHA referenced on CI/CD runner or developer host Delivery High
Hunts process executions whose command line embeds any of the known imposter commit SHAs that were tagged into tj-actions/changed-files (CVE-2025-30066) or the reviewdog action family (CVE-2025-30154). These SHAs only legitimately appear during the active compromise windows (Mar 11 18:42-20:31 UTC and Mar 14-15 2025); subsequent references mean either a workflow is still pinned to the malicious revision or someone is fetching it. Rationale: Cross-checked against Wiz, CISA AA25-077A, and the GitHub Advisory Database — the five SHAs in the article's IOC bundle are the confirmed imposter commits to which tj-actions/changed-files and the reviewdog actions' tags were pointed during the March 2025 compromise windows. Any process command line still embedding one of these SHAs (git checkout, runner-action invocation, gh api calls) means a pipeline is either still pinned to or fetching the malicious revision. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/ghsa-mrrh-fwg8-r2c3 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.wiz.io/blog/new-github-action-supply-chain-attack-reviewdog-action-setup • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-tj-actionschanged-files-cve-2025-30066-and-reviewdogaction • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/cve-2025-30154
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1199
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let ImposterShas = dynamic(["0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67","fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292","e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52","ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10","c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345"]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any (ImposterShas) or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any (ImposterShas)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
let ImposterShas = dynamic(["0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67","fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292","e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52","ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10","c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345"]);
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ActingProcessCommandLine has_any (ImposterShas) or TargetProcessCommandLine has_any (ImposterShas) or ParentProcessCommandLine has_any (ImposterShas)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 7f9a4c2e-3d8b-4f51-9e6a-1b2c3d4e5f60
status: experimental
description: Detects any process whose command line embeds a known imposter commit SHA from the March 2025 tj-actions/changed-files (CVE-2025-30066) or reviewdog (CVE-2025-30154) GitHub Actions supply-chain compromises.
references:

[LLM] tj-actions memdump.py credential-dump payload fetched or executed on CI runner Actions on Objectives High
Hunts the CVE-2025-30066 in-the-wild payload chain: a CI/CD runner (typically Linux ubuntu-latest) pulling https://gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965/raw/memdump.py and piping it to python3. Catches both the network egress shape and the process-tree shape (curl|python3 with the gist URL in cmdline) so it fires even if the runner blocks one telemetry source. Rationale: The gist URL https://gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965/raw/memdump.py and the curl|python3 invocation pattern are confirmed by Wiz, Sysdig, and Aqua as the exact in-the-wild payload of CVE-2025-30066 — a Python memory dumper that reads the GitHub Actions Runner.Worker process memory for secrets. The gist ID is unique and unrelated to legitimate GitHub gist traffic, so any reference to it on a runner is malicious. Cross-checked against: • https://www.wiz.io/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.sysdig.com/blog/detecting-and-mitigating-the-tj-actions-changed-files-supply-chain-attack-cve-2025-30066 • https://www.aquasec.com/blog/github-action-tj-actions-changed-files-compromised/ • https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/alerts/2025/03/18/supply-chain-compromise-third-party-tj-actionschanged-files-cve-2025-30066-and-reviewdogaction • https://github.com/advisories/ghsa-mrrh-fwg8-r2c3
ATT&CKT1552.001 T1003 T1567 T1059.006
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Web Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
let GistId = "30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965";
let ProcessHit = DeviceProcessEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("memdump.py", GistId, "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin")
     or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("memdump.py", GistId, "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin")
  | extend Signal = "process_cmdline"
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, Signal, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath;
let NetworkHit = DeviceNetworkEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where RemoteUrl has GistId or RemoteUrl has "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965"
  | extend Signal = "network_egress"
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, Signal, FileName=InitiatingProcessFileName, ProcessCommandLine=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath=InitiatingProcessFolderPath;
union ProcessHit, NetworkHit
| order by Timestamp desc
let GistId = "30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965";
let ProcHit = ImProcessCreate
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where ActingProcessCommandLine has_any ("memdump.py", GistId, "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin")
     or TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("memdump.py", GistId, "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin")
  | extend Signal = "process_cmdline"
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, Signal, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine;
let WebHit = ImWebSession
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where Url has GistId or Url has "gist.githubusercontent.com/nikitastupin/30e525b776c409e03c2d6f328f254965"
  | extend Signal = "web_egress"
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername=SrcUsername, Signal, ActingProcessName="", ActingProcessCommandLine=Url, TargetProcessName="", TargetProcessCommandLine=HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent;
union ProcHit, WebHit
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High

ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-30066", "CVE-2025-30154")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-30066", "CVE-2025-30154")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345") or SHA1 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345") or MD5 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345") or SHA1 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345") or MD5 in~ ("0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["0e58ed8671d6b60d0890c21b07f8835ace038e67", "fbc2c5ebe64389f297a7808025379f77133f1292", "e1e36574b3af1ddaab74f5e69505d8836bf12f52", "ce4a123414f9fffa959d1f329c4749da83c4bf10", "c17ac4b5c1cb901a7ccddf00ac9722b8e2725345"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

// Article-specific bespoke detection — The GitHub Warning Everyone Ignores: 'This Commit Does Not Belong to Any Branch'
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.

       Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.file_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when

references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/

        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'

            - '\mshta.exe'

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender

Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;

// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.

    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"

    | where isnotempty(MessageId)

              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;

   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"

status: experimental
description: |

    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.

    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/

            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium

Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2020-25078", "CVE-2017-7921", "CVE-2018-9995", "CVE-2021-33044", "CVE-2021-36260")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2020-25078", "CVE-2017-7921", "CVE-2018-9995", "CVE-2021-33044", "CVE-2021-36260")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2025-20337", "CVE-2025-20281", "CVE-2025-20282", "CVE-2025-5777")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] npm registry typosquat npnjs.com — DNS / URL click (eslint-config-prettier maintainer phishing kit) · [LLM] Scavenger Loader DLL (node-gyp.dll) written inside node_modules of CVE-2025-54313 packages · Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] rundll32.exe loading node-gyp.dll dropped by Scavenger-infected npm postinstall (CVE-2025-54313) · [LLM] Scavenger Stealer sandbox-evasion marker file %TEMP%\SCVNGR_VM created · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Scavenger Stealer C2 beacon to corroborated infrastructure (datahog.su / datalytica.su / smartscreen-api.com) · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] rundll32.exe loading node-gyp.dll dropped by Scavenger-infected npm postinstall (CVE-2025-54313) Installation High
Detects the smoking-gun payload of the eslint-config-prettier / Scavenger Stealer supply-chain compromise: an npm install context (node.exe / npm / yarn / pnpm executing install.js) spawning rundll32.exe to load node-gyp.dll. Legitimate node-gyp is a Node.js native module — it is NEVER loaded via rundll32 in normal use, so the combination is a deterministic IOC. Rationale: Pivot is the deterministic install.js → rundll32.exe node-gyp.dll chain documented in the Snyk article and the CVE-2025-54313 description, with the parent-process narrowed to npm-family binaries. Cross-checked against Invoke-RE, SafeDep, StepSecurity and the NVD CVE record — all four describe the same rundll32 + node-gyp.dll command construction. Cross-checked against: • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-54313 • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-eslint-config-prettier-npm-package-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://safedep.io/eslint-config-prettier-major-npm-supply-chain-hack/ • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/supply-chain-security-alert-eslint-config-prettier-package-shows-signs-of-compromise • https://www.crowdstrike.com/en-us/blog/crowdstrike-falcon-prevents-npm-package-supply-chain-attacks/
ATT&CKT1218.011 T1195.002 T1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents

| where FileName =~ "rundll32.exe"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "node-gyp.dll"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","npm.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","cmd.exe")
    or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("install.js","npm-cli.js","npm.cmd"," yarn "," pnpm ","node_modules")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          GrandparentImage = InitiatingProcessParentFileName,
          SHA256, FolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith @"\rundll32.exe"
| where CommandLine has "node-gyp.dll"
| where ParentProcessName has_any (@"\node.exe", @"\npm.exe", @"\yarn.exe", @"\pnpm.exe", @"\cmd.exe")
    or CommandLine has_any ("install.js","npm-cli.js","node_modules")
| where SubjectUserName !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account = SubjectUserName,
          ChildCmd = CommandLine,
          ParentImage = ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Scavenger Stealer C2 beacon to corroborated infrastructure (datahog.su / datalytica.su / smartscreen-api.com) Command & Control High
Detects outbound DNS or network connections to the six Scavenger Stealer C2 domains corroborated across Invoke-RE, SafeDep, and CrowdStrike Falcon write-ups. Any contact from an endpoint to this set is post-execution evidence that the npm payload reached its second stage and is exfiltrating .npmrc tokens / browser data. Rationale: Domain list is the verbatim IOC set published by Invoke-RE and mirrored in the public GitHub research repo and StepSecurity / SafeDep advisories. `firebase.su` and `smartscreen-api.com` are typosquats of Google Firebase and Microsoft SmartScreen — no legitimate enterprise reason to resolve them. `.su` TLD is rare enough in modern enterprise traffic that the false-positive surface is near-zero. Cross-checked against: • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-eslint-config-prettier-npm-package-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://github.com/Invoke-RE/community-malware-research/blob/main/Research/Loaders/Scavenger/IOCs.md • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/supply-chain-security-alert-eslint-config-prettier-package-shows-signs-of-compromise • https://safedep.io/eslint-config-prettier-major-npm-supply-chain-hack/
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1041 T1568
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
let ScavengerC2 = dynamic(["datahog.su","datalytica.su","datacrab-analytics.com","dieorsuffer.com","firebase.su","smartscreen-api.com"]);
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(60d)
| extend Host = tolower(tostring(parse_url(RemoteUrl).Host))
| where Host has_any (ScavengerC2)
    or RemoteUrl has_any (ScavengerC2)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName = InitiatingProcessAccountName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
let ScavengerC2 = dynamic(["datahog.su","datalytica.su","datacrab-analytics.com","dieorsuffer.com","firebase.su","smartscreen-api.com"]);
union isfuzzy=true
    (ImDnsActivity
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
        | where DnsQuery has_any (ScavengerC2)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, SrcUsername, Indicator = DnsQuery, Kind = "dns"),
    (ImNetworkSession
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
        | where (isnotempty(Url) and Url has_any (ScavengerC2))
             or (isnotempty(DstHostname) and DstHostname has_any (ScavengerC2))
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, SrcUsername, Indicator = coalesce(Url, DstHostname), Kind = "network")
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] npm registry typosquat npnjs.com — DNS / URL click (eslint-config-prettier maintainer phishing kit) Delivery High
Detects DNS lookups, URL clicks, or web sessions to npnjs.com — the typosquat domain used in the spear-phishing email (spoofing support@npmjs.org) that harvested the npm maintainer credentials behind CVE-2025-54313. Hits here are pre-compromise evidence that an internal maintainer or developer is in the kill chain. Rationale: npnjs.com is the specific typosquat domain named in the Snyk article (a single-character substitution of npmjs.com — `m` → `n`). Cross-confirmed by BleepingComputer, CSO Online, and SafeDep coverage of the JounQin maintainer compromise. The domain has no legitimate purpose; any resolution from inside the org is a direct indicator the phishing kit reached a developer mailbox. Cross-checked against: • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/popular-npm-linter-packages-hijacked-via-phishing-to-drop-malware/ • https://www.csoonline.com/article/4026380/prettier-eslint-npm-packages-hijacked-in-a-sophisticated-supply-chain-attack.html • https://safedep.io/eslint-config-prettier-major-npm-supply-chain-hack/

[LLM] Scavenger Loader DLL (node-gyp.dll) written inside node_modules of CVE-2025-54313 packages Delivery High
Detects the file-write of a node-gyp.dll PE32+ payload inside the node_modules\ tree of any of the seven packages compromised in CVE-2025-54313: eslint-config-prettier, eslint-plugin-prettier, synckit, @pkgr/core, napi-postinstall, got-fetch, is. Legitimate node-gyp ships as JavaScript and Python build helpers — there is no upstream node-gyp.dll. The presence of this PE inside the package directory is deterministic evidence of a compromised tarball. Rationale: Detection anchored on the seven compromised package names from the Snyk article + the unique node-gyp.dll filename that the malicious tarballs ship. The combination (node_modules path component + specific package name + node-gyp.dll filename) is deterministic — there's no legitimate node-gyp.dll in the real npm distribution. SHA-256 hashes of the known Scavenger Loader samples can be fed into the response runbook to confirm. Cross-checked against: • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-54313 • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-eslint-config-prettier-npm-package-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://github.com/Invoke-RE/community-malware-research/blob/main/Research/Loaders/Scavenger/IOCs.md • https://socket.dev/blog/npm-is-package-hijacked-in-expanding-supply-chain-attack

[LLM] Scavenger Stealer sandbox-evasion marker file %TEMP%\SCVNGR_VM created Installation High
Detects creation of the SCVNGR_VM marker file in %TEMP% — Scavenger's anti-analysis / VM-detection breadcrumb dropped by the loader during execution. The string is unique to this malware family and is extremely unlikely to appear anywhere else on a corporate endpoint. Rationale: SCVNGR_VM is the unique sandbox-evasion marker filename documented in the Invoke-RE deep-dive — written by the Scavenger DLL on first run. The string is six characters of malware-specific shorthand with no English-word collision; substring presence anywhere on disk is near-zero false-positive. Cross-checked against: • https://invokere.com/posts/2025/07/scavenger-malware-distributed-via-eslint-config-prettier-npm-package-supply-chain-compromise/ • https://github.com/Invoke-RE/community-malware-research/blob/main/Research/Loaders/Scavenger/IOCs.md
ATT&CKT1497 T1036
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(60d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where FolderPath has "SCVNGR_VM" or FileName has "SCVNGR_VM"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName = InitiatingProcessAccountName,
          FolderPath, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessParentFileName, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where TargetFilePath has "SCVNGR_VM" or TargetFileName has "SCVNGR_VM"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName,
          ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-54313")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-54313")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High
Attachment delivery + execution by recipient — most common malware initial access.
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1204.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                      "mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
| join kind=inner MalAttachments on $left.AccountUpn == $right.RecipientEmailAddress
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: FakeCaptcha / ClickFix - Clipboard-Injected PowerShell from Explorer
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d06
status: experimental
description: |
    "FakeCaptcha" / "ClickFix" social-engineering: malicious site asks
    user to press Win+R and paste a payload. The result is
    powershell/mshta launched directly from explorer.exe with iex /
    base64 / downloadstring in the command line.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1204/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1204.002
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_parent:
        ParentImage|endswith:
            - '\explorer.exe'
            - '\RuntimeBroker.exe'
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
    selection_cmd:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'iex'
            - 'invoke-expression'
            - 'frombase64'
            - 'downloadstring'
            - 'hxxp'
            - 'curl '
            - 'wget '
    condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
    - IT-driven scripted automation that admins paste-launch
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("explorer.exe","RuntimeBroker.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*iex*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*hxxp*" OR Processes.process="*curl*" OR Processes.process="*wget*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("explorer.exe","RuntimeBroker.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","mshta.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*iex*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*hxxp*" OR Processes.process="*curl*" OR Processes.process="*wget*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02

    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access

    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:

      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("c68e42f416f482d43653f36cd14384270b54b68d6496a8e34ce887687de5b441") or SHA1 in~ ("c68e42f416f482d43653f36cd14384270b54b68d6496a8e34ce887687de5b441") or MD5 in~ ("c68e42f416f482d43653f36cd14384270b54b68d6496a8e34ce887687de5b441")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("c68e42f416f482d43653f36cd14384270b54b68d6496a8e34ce887687de5b441") or SHA1 in~ ("c68e42f416f482d43653f36cd14384270b54b68d6496a8e34ce887687de5b441") or MD5 in~ ("c68e42f416f482d43653f36cd14384270b54b68d6496a8e34ce887687de5b441")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["c68e42f416f482d43653f36cd14384270b54b68d6496a8e34ce887687de5b441"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

CISA KEV
2025-07-22 6 use cases 1 technique 5 kill-chain phases detected

Microsoft SharePoint contains an improper authentication vulnerability that allows an authorized attacker to perform spoofing over a network. Known ransomware use: Known. Federal patch due: 2025-07-23.

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change · LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) · Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-49706", "CVE-2025-49704", "CVE-2025-53770", "CVE-2025-53771")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-49706", "CVE-2025-49704", "CVE-2025-53770", "CVE-2025-53771")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2025-49706", "CVE-2025-49704", "CVE-2025-53770", "CVE-2025-53771")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity

    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities
